	composition law for $\mathcal{A}$:
	\begin{prooftree}
		\def\ \geq\ {\ \sqsubseteq\ }
		\alwaysDoubleLine
		\Axiom$\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a^\prime)\ \geq\  [\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)]$
		\UnaryInf$\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a^\prime)\otimes  \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,b)\ \geq\  \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)$
	\end{prooftree}
\item[[$\overline{Y}(a)$ is a copresheaf on $\mathcal{A}\!\!\!$]]
	The claim is 
	\begin{align*}
		\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(b,b^\prime) &\sqsubseteq
		[\overline{Y}(a)(b),\overline{Y}(a)(b^\prime)] \\
		&= [\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)],
	\end{align*}
	and this is equivalent to an instance of the 
	composition law for $\mathcal{A}$:
	\begin{prooftree}
		\def\ \geq\ {\ \sqsubseteq\ }
		\alwaysDoubleLine
		\Axiom$\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(b,b^\prime)\ \geq\  [\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)]$
		\UnaryInf$\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(b,b^\prime)\otimes  \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)\ \geq\  \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)$
		\UnaryInf$\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,b)\otimes  \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(b,b^\prime)\ \geq\  \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)$
	\end{prooftree}
\end{description}
Next we show that $Y$ and $\overline{Y}$ are 
fully faithful $\mathcal{V}$-functors:
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[$Y$ is a fully faithful $\mathcal{V}$-functor\!\!\!]]
	The claim is 
	\begin{align*}
		\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(b,b^\prime) &=
		\textup{Hom}_{\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\mathcal{V})}(Y(b),Y(b^\prime)) \\
		&= \bigwedge_{a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
		\left\{[Y(b)(a),Y(b^\prime)(a)]\right\} \\
		&= \bigwedge_{a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
		\left\{[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)]\right\}.
	\end{align*}
	We prove this by showing the two inequalities:
	\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
	\item[[\mbox{$\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(b,b^\prime)
	\sqsubseteq \bigwedge_{a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
	\left\{[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)]\right\}$}\!\!\!]]
	It is equivalent to 
	\[
	\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(b,b^\prime)
	\sqsubseteq [\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)]\quad(\forall a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}))
	\]
	and its proof for some fixed $a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})$
	is already given above when we prove that
	$\overline{Y}(a)$ is a copresheaf on $\mathcal{A}$.
	\item[[\mbox{$\bigwedge_{a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
	\left\{[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)]\right\}
	\sqsubseteq \textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(b,b^\prime)$}\!\!\!]]
	First note that
	\[
	\bigwedge_{a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
	\left\{[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b^\prime)]\right\}\sqsubseteq
	[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(b,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(b,b^\prime)]
	\]
	holds.
	Using an instance of the identity law for $\mathcal{A}$,
	$e\sqsubseteq \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(b,b)$,
	we have the following:
	\begin{align*}
	[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(b,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(b,b^\prime)]
	&\sqsubseteq [e,\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(b,b^\prime)]\\
	&= \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(b,b^\prime).
	\end{align*}
	Therefore the claim follows.
	\end{description}
\item[[$\overline{Y}$ is a fully faithful $\mathcal{V}$-functor\!\!\!]]
	The claim is 
	\begin{align*}
		\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a^\prime) &=
		\textup{Hom}_{\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{V})}(\overline{Y}(a^\prime),\overline{Y}(a)) \\
		&= \bigwedge_{b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
		\left\{[\overline{Y}(a^\prime)(b),\overline{Y}(a)(b)]\right\} \\
		&= \bigwedge_{b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
		\left\{[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)]\right\}.
	\end{align*}
	We prove this by showing the two inequalities:
	\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
	\item[[\mbox{$\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a^\prime)
	\sqsubseteq \bigwedge_{b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
	\left\{[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)]\right\}$}\!\!\!]]
	It is equivalent to 
	\[
	\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a^\prime)
	\sqsubseteq [\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)]\quad(\forall b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}))
	\]
	and its proof for some fixed $b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})$
	is already given above when we prove that
	$Y(b)$ is a presheaf on $\mathcal{A}$.
	\item[[\mbox{$\bigwedge_{b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
	\left\{[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)]\right\}
	\sqsubseteq \textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a^\prime)$}\!\!\!]]
	First note that
	\[
	\bigwedge_{b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}
	\left\{[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,b),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)]\right\}\sqsubseteq
	[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,a^\prime),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,a^\prime)]
	\]
	holds.
	Using an instance of the identity law for $\mathcal{A}$,
	$e\sqsubseteq \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,a^\prime)$,
	we have the following:
	\begin{align*}
	[\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a^\prime,a^\prime),\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,a^\prime)]
	&\sqsubseteq [e,\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,a^\prime)]\\
	&= \textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,a^\prime).
	\end{align*}
	Therefore the claim follows.
	\end{description}
\end{description}
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- defn-11: body_md -->
The $\mathcal{V}$-functor $Y$ is called the 
\textbf{Yoneda embedding}, and 
$\overline{Y}$ the \textbf{co-Yoneda embedding}.
<!-- end defn-11 -->
<!-- ex-27: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),\preceq_\mathcal{A})$ 
be a $\textbf{2}$-category. 
Under the identification of presheaves on $\mathcal{A}$
and lower sets of $\mathcal{A}$,
the Yoneda embedding turns out to be the well-known
\textit{principal ideal} operation 
$\mathord{\downarrow}(-)$:
\begin{align*}
\textup{Ob}(Y(b))^{-1}\big(\{\textup{true}\}\big)&=\mathord{\downarrow}(b)\\
&=\{a\in\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})\mid a\preceq_\mathcal{A} b \}.
\end{align*}
Dually, the co-Yoneda embedding corresponds to the
\textit{principal filter} operation $\mathord{\uparrow}(-)$:
\begin{align*}
\textup{Ob}(\overline{Y}(a))^{-1}\big(\{\textup{true}\}\big)&=\mathord{\uparrow} (a)\\
&=\{b\in\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})\mid a\preceq_\mathcal{A} b \}.
\end{align*}

Therefore, the Yoneda embedding theorem specializes to
the statement that 
\textit{every preordered set embeds into the poset of 
its lower subsets (ordered by the inclusion) and 
the poset of its upper subsets (ordered by the 
opposite of the inclusion)}:
\begin{align*}
b\preceq_\mathcal{A} b^\prime 
&\iff \mathord{\downarrow}(b)\subseteq\mathord{\downarrow} (b^\prime);\\
a\preceq_\mathcal{A} a^\prime 
&\iff \mathord{\uparrow}(a)\supseteq\mathord{\uparrow} (a^\prime).
\end{align*}
But note that such \textit{embeddings} are not 
injective unless the preorder is an order.
<!-- end ex-27 -->
<!-- ex-28: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ 
be a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}_+$-category. 
The Yoneda embedding $Y$ sends a point $b$ of 
$\mathcal{A}$ to the ``distance to $b$'' function:
\[
	\textup{Ob}(Y(b))=\lambda a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}^\textup{op}).\,d_\mathcal{A}(a,b).
\]
The co-Yoneda embedding $\overline{Y}$ sends a 
point $a$ of $\mathcal{A}$ to the ``distance from $a$''
function:
\[
	\textup{Ob}(\overline{Y}(a))=\lambda b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}).\,d_\mathcal{A}(a,b).
\]

The Yoneda embedding theorem reads:
\begin{align*}
d_\mathcal{A}(b,b^\prime)
&=d_{\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\overline{\mathbb{K}}_+)}
(Y(b),Y(b^\prime));\\
d_\mathcal{A}(a,a^\prime)
&=d_{{\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}_+)}^\textup{op}}
(\overline{Y}(a),\overline{Y}(a^\prime)).
\end{align*}
If $\mathcal{A}$ is a classical metric space, 
then by symmetry $Y$ and $\overline{Y}$ coincide and 
turn out to be the so-called 
\textit{Fr\'{e}chet embedding}.
<!-- end ex-28 -->
<!-- ex-29: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ 
be a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-category. We have
\[
	\textup{Ob}(Y(b))=\lambda a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}^\textup{op}).\,d_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\quad
	\textup{Ob}(\overline{Y}(a))=\lambda b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}).\,d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
\]
and 
\[
d_\mathcal{A}(b,b^\prime)
=d_{\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\overline{\mathbb{K}})}
(Y(b),Y(b^\prime)),\quad
d_\mathcal{A}(a,a^\prime)
=d_{{\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}})}^\textup{op}}
(\overline{Y}(a),\overline{Y}(a^\prime)).
\]
<!-- end ex-29 -->
<!-- ex-30: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ 
be a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}_+^\text{Cart}$-category. 
We have
\[
	\textup{Ob}(Y(b))=\lambda a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}^\textup{op}).\,d_\mathcal{A}(a,b),\quad
	\textup{Ob}(\overline{Y}(a))=\lambda b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}).\,d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
\]
and 
\[
d_\mathcal{A}(b,b^\prime)
=d_{\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\overline{\mathbb{K}}_+^\text{Cart})}
(Y(b),Y(b^\prime)),\quad
d_\mathcal{A}(a,a^\prime)
=d_{{\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}_+^\text{Cart})}^\textup{op}}
(\overline{Y}(a),\overline{Y}(a^\prime)).
\]
<!-- end ex-30 -->
<!-- defn-12: body_md -->
A $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$\textbf{-extended L-convex set}
$\mathbf{D}$ is a pair 
$({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$ where
\begin{itemize}
\item 
${\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$ is a set called the \textbf{index set} 
of $\mathbf{D}$;
\item
$\mathbf{D}_0$ is a subset of 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}^{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$
called the \textbf{underlying set} of $\mathbf{D}$;
\end{itemize}
such that the following axioms hold:
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
	\item[$\qquad$(Order completeness)]
	$\bigvee_{p \in S} p,\ 
	\bigwedge_{p \in S} p\in \mathbf{D}_0
	\quad(\forall S\subseteq \mathbf{D}_0)$;
	\item[$\qquad$(Weight completeness)]
	$p+\alpha \cdot\textbf{1}\in \mathbf{D}_0\quad 
	(\forall p\in \mathbf{D}_0,\ \forall \alpha \in \mathbb{K}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\})$.
\end{description}
<!-- end defn-12 -->
<!-- prop-10: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$ be a
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex set.
Then the following hold:
\begin{align}\label{eq:weightminus}
	p-\alpha \cdot\mathbf{1}\in \mathbf{D}_0\quad 
	(\forall p\in \mathbf{D}_0,\ \forall \alpha \in \mathbb{K}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}).
\end{align}
<!-- end prop-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
When $\alpha \in \mathbb{K}$, then clearly 
\[
p-\alpha \cdot\mathbf{1}=p+(-\alpha)\cdot \mathbf{1}.
\]
If $\alpha =-\infty$, then 
\[
(p-(-\infty)\cdot\mathbf{1})(v)=
\begin{cases}
	-\infty &(p(v)=-\infty)\\
	\infty &(\text{otherwise})
\end{cases}
\quad (\forall v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}),
\]
and this is realized as 
\[
\bigwedge_{\alpha\in\mathbb{K}}(p+\alpha\cdot\mathbf{1})=\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{K}}\{p+\alpha\cdot\mathbf{1}\}.
\]
Finally, if $\alpha =\infty$, then 
\[
(p-\infty\cdot\mathbf{1})(v)=-\infty\quad (\forall v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})})
\]
and this is written as 
\[
\bigwedge \emptyset=\sup\emptyset.
\]
Hence, in either case 
$(p-\alpha\cdot\mathbf{1})\in\mathbf{D}_0$ by the order and 
weight completeness of $\mathbf{D}$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- defn-13: body_md -->
Let $V$ be a \textit{finite} set.
A subset $D\subseteq \mathbb{Z}^V$ is an
\textbf{L-convex set} if the following hold:
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
	\item[$\qquad$(Nonemptiness)]
	$D\neq \emptyset$;
	\item[$\qquad$(SBS\mbox{$[\mathbb{Z}]$})]
	$p\land q,\ p\lor q\in D
	\quad(\forall p,q\in D)$;
	\item[$\qquad$(TRS\mbox{$[\mathbb{Z}]$})]
	$p+\alpha \cdot\textbf{1}\in D\quad (\forall p\in D,\ \forall \alpha \in \mathbb{Z})$.
\end{description}
<!-- end defn-13 -->
<!-- defn-14: body_md -->
Let $V$ be a \textit{finite} set.
A subset $D\subseteq \mathbb{R}^V$ is an
\textbf{L-convex polyhedron} if the following hold:
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
	\item[$\qquad$(Nonemptiness)]
	$D\neq \emptyset$;
	\item[$\qquad$(Closedness)]
	$D$ is a closed subset of $\mathbb{R}^V$;
	\item[$\qquad$(SBS\mbox{$[\mathbb{R}]$})]
	$p\land q,\ p\lor q\in D
	\quad(\forall p,q\in D)$;
	\item[$\qquad$(TRS\mbox{$[\mathbb{R}]$})]
	$p+\alpha \cdot\textbf{1}\in D\quad (\forall p\in D,\ \forall \alpha \in \mathbb{R})$.
\end{description}
<!-- end defn-14 -->
<!-- ex-31: body_md -->
The pair 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}=(\{\ast\},\mathbb{K}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\})$, 
where $\{\ast\}$ is a singleton and  
$\mathbb{K}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}$ is a 
(non-proper) subset of 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}^{\{\ast\}}$, is clearly a 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex set.
<!-- end ex-31 -->
<!-- defn-15: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$
and $\mathbf{E}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})},\mathbf{E}_0)$ be 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex sets.
A \textbf{homomorphism}
\[
\Phi\colon \mathbf{D}\longrightarrow \mathbf{E}
\] 
from $\mathbf{D}$ to $\mathbf{E}$ is a map
\[
{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}\colon {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}\longrightarrow {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}
\]
from the index set of $\mathbf{E}$ to 
that of $\mathbf{D}$, such that
for all $p\in \mathbf{D}_0$, the element 
$\Phi_0(p)\in \overline{\mathbb{K}}^{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}$ 
given by
\[
\Phi_0(p)=p\circ {\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}\colon {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}\overset{{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}}{\longrightarrow} {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow} \mathbb{K}\cup \{-\infty,\infty\}
\]
is an element of $\mathbf{E}_0$.

$\mathbf{D}$ is called the \textbf{domain} of $\Phi$ and
$\mathbf{E}$ the \textbf{codomain} of $\Phi$. 
We denote the set of all homomorphisms
from $\mathbf{D}$ to $\mathbf{E}$ by 
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\mathbf{E} \rrbracket}_0$.
<!-- end defn-15 -->
<!-- defn-16: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf{D}$ and $\mathbf{E}$ be 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex sets.
A homomorphism 
\[
\Phi\colon \mathbf{D}\longrightarrow \mathbf{E}
\] 
from $\mathbf{D}$ to $\mathbf{E}$
is an \textbf{isomorphism} 
if there exists a homomorphism 
(called its \textbf{inverse})
\[
\Psi\colon \mathbf{E}\longrightarrow \mathbf{D}
\]
from $\mathbf{E}$ to $\mathbf{D}$ such that
$\Psi\circ\Phi=1_{\mathbf{D}}$ and 
$\Phi\circ\Psi=1_{\mathbf{E}}$
hold, where $1_{\mathbf{D}}$ and $1_{\mathbf{E}}$ are the
identity homomorphisms on $\mathbf{D}$ and $\mathbf{E}$,
respectively.

If there is an isomorphism between 
$\mathbf{D}$ and $\mathbf{E}$, then they are said to be 
\textbf{isomorphic} and written as 
$\mathbf{D}\cong\mathbf{E}$.
<!-- end defn-16 -->
<!-- defn-17: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$
and $\mathbf{E}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})},\mathbf{E}_0)$ be 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex sets.
The set ${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\mathbf{E} \rrbracket}$ of all
homomorphisms from $\mathbf{D}$ to $\mathbf{E}$ admits
a canonical relation $\Rightarrow$ defined as follows:
\[
	\Phi\Rightarrow\Psi\iff\Phi_0(p)\geq\Psi_0(p)\quad(\forall p\in\mathbf{D}_0),
\]
where $\Phi,\Psi\colon\mathbf{D}\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}$
are homomorphisms and 
$\geq$ is the coordinate-wise order on $\mathbf{E}_0$.
We call $\Rightarrow$ the \textbf{canonical ordering}
on ${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\mathbf{E} \rrbracket}_0$.
<!-- end defn-17 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{mainthm_ob}
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ be
a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-category and
$\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$
be a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex set.
Then the following hold:
\begin{enumerate}
\item
$\mathcal{A}\cong {\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$.
\item
$\mathbf{D}\cong [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- lem-1: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ be
a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-category.
Then the pair 
$[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),$ 
$[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0)$, 
where 
$[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$
is the set of all nonexpansive maps from $\mathcal{A}$ to
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$ 
(as a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-category),
seen as a subset of 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}^{\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}$, 
is a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended
L-convex set.
<!-- end lem-1 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[Order completeness\!\!\!]]
Let $S$ be a set of nonexpansive maps from
$\mathcal{A}$ to $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$.
The claim is that $\bigvee_{p\in S} p$, 
$\bigwedge_{p\in S}p$
are again nonexpansive maps from $\mathcal{A}$ to
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$.
In other words, that
\begin{align}
d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
&\geq d_{\overline{\mathbb{K}}}\big(\bigvee_{p\in S} p(a),
\bigvee_{p\in S} p(b)\big)
=\inf_{p\in S} \{p(b)\}-\inf_{p\in S} \{p(a)\},\label{oc_inf}\\
d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
&\geq d_{\overline{\mathbb{K}}}\big(\bigwedge_{p\in S} p(a),\bigwedge_{p\in S} p(b)\big)
=\sup_{p\in S} \{p(b)\}-\sup_{p\in S} \{p(a)\}\label{oc_sup}
\end{align}
hold for all $a,b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})$.
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[(\ref{oc_inf})\!\!\!]]
For each fixed $p^\prime\in S$, the increasing condition
implies
\[
	d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)\geq p^\prime(b)-p^\prime(a)
	\geq \inf_{p\in S} \{p(b)\}-p^\prime (a).
\]
By taking $\sup$ with respect to $p^\prime\in S$,
we have
\[
	d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)\geq \sup_{p^\prime\in S}
	\left\{\inf_{p\in S}\{p(b)\}-p^\prime(a)\right\}
	=\inf_{p\in S} \{p(b)\}-\inf_{p\in S} \{p(a)\},
\]
where the equality follows from an instance of
a statement in
Proposition \ref{prop:lim_ten_hom} that
\[
\inf_{p\in S} \{p(b)\}-(-)
\]
turns suprema ($\inf$'s) into infima ($\sup$'s).
\item[[(\ref{oc_sup})\!\!\!]]
For each fixed $p^\prime\in S$, the increasing condition
implies
\[
	d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)\geq p^\prime(b)-p^\prime(a)
	\geq p^\prime(b)-\sup_{p\in S} \{p(a)\}.
\]
By taking $\sup$ with respect to $p^\prime\in S$,
we have
\[
	d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)\geq \sup_{p^\prime\in S}
	\left\{p^\prime(b)-\sup_{p\in S} \{p(a)\}\right\}
	=\sup_{p\in S} \{p(b)\}-\sup_{p\in S} \{p(a)\},
\]
where the equality follows from an instance of
a statement in
Proposition \ref{prop:SMCCL} that 
\[
(-)-\sup_{p\in S} \{p(a)\}
\]
preserves infima ($\sup$'s).
\end{description}
\item[[Weight completeness (for $+$)\!\!\!]]
Let $p$ be a nonexpansive map from $\mathcal{A}$ to 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$ and 
$\alpha\in \mathbb{K}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}$.
The claim is that $p+\alpha\cdot\textbf{1}$
is again a nonexpansive map 
from $\mathcal{A}$ to $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$.
In other words, that 
\begin{align*}
d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)&\geq d_{\overline{\mathbb{K}}}\big(p(a)+\alpha,p(b)+\alpha\big)
=\big(p(b)+\alpha\big)-\big(p(a)+\alpha\big)
\end{align*}
hold for all $a,b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})$.
We use the increasing condition for $p$
\[
	d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)\geq p(b)-p(a)
\]
and show 
\[
p(b)-p(a)\geq \big(p(b)+\alpha\big)-\big(p(a)+\alpha\big),
\]
which has an equivalent formula:
\begin{prooftree}
	\def\ \geq\ {\ \geq\ }
	\alwaysDoubleLine
	\Axiom$p(b)-p(a)\ \geq\  \big(p(b)+\alpha\big)-\big(p(a)+\alpha\big)$
	\UnaryInf$\big(p(b)-p(a)\big)+\big(p(a)+\alpha\big) \ \geq\  p(b)+\alpha$
	\UnaryInf$\big(\big(p(b)-p(a)\big)+p(a)\big)+\alpha \ \geq\  p(b)+\alpha$
\end{prooftree}
Therefore it suffices to prove 
$\big(p(b)-p(a)\big)+p(a)\geq p(b)$, as follows:
\begin{prooftree}
	\def\ \geq\ {\ \geq\ }
	\alwaysDoubleLine
	\Axiom$\big(p(b)-p(a)\big)+p(a)\ \geq\  p(b)$
	\UnaryInf$p(b)-p(a) \ \geq\  p(b)-p(a)$
\end{prooftree}
\end{description}
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lem-2: body_md -->
\label{lcs2kcat_ob}
Let $\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$
be a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex set.
Then the pair
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}=\big({\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0,d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}\big)$,
where
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0$ is the set of all 
homomorphisms from $\mathbf{D}$ to $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$
(as a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex set)
and $d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}$  
is the distance function on it defined by the 
sup-distance between the underlying maps, 
is a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-category.
<!-- end lem-2 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
First note that elements of 
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0$ 
can canonically be identified with that of 
${\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$, 
via a bijection $\pi_\mathbf{D}$ defined as follows:
\[
	\pi_\mathbf{D}\colon {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}\longrightarrow {\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0,
	\qquad 
	\pi_\mathbf{D}(v)=\pi_v
	\quad (\forall v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}),
\]
where
\[
	{\textup{Ind}(\pi_v)}\colon \{\ast\}\longrightarrow {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\qquad
	{\textup{Ind}(\pi_v)}(\ast)=v.
\]
The underlying maps of $\pi_v$'s are given as the 
``projection maps'':
\[
(\pi_v)_0\colon \mathbf{D}_0\longrightarrow \mathbb{K}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}\subseteq \overline{\mathbb{K}}^{\{\ast\}}\qquad
(\pi_v)_0(p)=p(v).
\]
See Fig.~\ref{fig:lcsproj} for an illustration.
Thus we may write ${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0=\{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}$.
Also note that 
\begin{align}
d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_w)
&=\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{d_{\overline{\mathbb{K}}}((\pi_v)_0(p),(\pi_w)_0(p))\}\nonumber\\
&=\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{d_{\overline{\mathbb{K}}}(p(v),p(w))\}\nonumber\\
&=\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(v)\}.\label{d_dk}
\end{align}
\begin{figure}
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}
      
\node at (0,5.5) {$\mathbf{D}$};
\node at (7.5,5.5) {$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$};
\draw [my arrow] (0.5,5.5) -- (7,5.5);
\node at (3.75,5.5)[anchor=south] {$\pi_v$};
           
\node at (0,4.25) {${\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$};
\node at (7.5,4.25) {$\{\ast\}$};
\node at (0,3.8) {$\vdots$};
\node at (0,3.3) {$v$};
\node at (7.5,3.3) {$\ast$};
\node at (0,3) {$\vdots$};
\draw [my mapsto] (7,3.3) -- (0.5,3.3);
\draw [my arrow] (6.9,4.25) -- (0.8,4.25);
\node at (3.75,4.25) [anchor=south]{${\textup{Ind}(\pi_v)}$};
        
\draw [my mapsto] (2.5,0) -- (7,0);
\draw [my arrow] (0.5,2.25) -- (6.1,2.25);
\node at (3.75,2.25)[anchor=south] {$(\pi_v)_0$};
\node at (0,2.25) {$\mathbf{D}_0$};
\node at (7.5,2.25) {$\mathbb{K}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}$};

\begin{scope}      
                      
\draw [my grid] (-2,-2) rectangle (2,2);
\draw [my grid,my coordarrow] (-1.6,0) -- (1.6,0);
\draw [my grid,my coordarrow] (0,-1.6) -- (0,1.6) node[anchor=east,my cood] {$v$};
                                               
                  
\newcommand{1.0}{1.0}
\draw [my frame,dashed] (-1.5,-1.0) .. 
      controls (-1.8,-1.0-0.3) 
      and (-2,-1.0-0.5) .. 
      (-2,-2);
\draw [my frame] (-1.5,-1.0) -- (1.0,1.5);
\draw [my frame,dashed] (1.0,1.5) .. 
      controls (1.0+0.3,1.8) 
      and (1.0+0.5,2) .. 
      (2,2);
                                               
                  
\newcommand{0.4}{0.4}
\draw [my frame,dashed] (-0.4,-1.5) ..
      controls (-0.4-0.3,-1.8)
      and (-0.4-0.5,-2) ..
      (-2,-2);
\draw [my frame] (-0.4,-1.5) -- (1.5,0.4);
\draw [my frame,dashed] (1.5,0.4) ..
      controls (1.8,0.4+0.3)
      and (2,0.4+0.5) ..
      (2,2);
                  
\begin{scope}[on background layer]
\fill [black!20!white] 
      (-2,-2) ..controls(-2,-1.0-0.5)and(-1.8,-1.0-0.3).. 
      (-1.5,-1.0) -- 
      (1.0,1.5) ..controls(1.0+0.3,1.8)and(1.0+0.5,2).. 
      (2,2) ..controls(2,0.4+0.5)and(1.8,0.4+0.3)..
      (1.5,0.4) --
      (-0.4,-1.5) ..controls(-0.4-0.3,-1.8)and(-0.4-0.5,-2)..
      (-2,-2);
\end{scope}
             
\filldraw [black] (-2,-2) circle (1.5pt)
          (2,2) circle (1.5pt);
\end{scope}

\begin{scope}[shift={(7.5,0)}]      
\draw [my frame,dashed] (0,-1.5) -- (0,-2);
\draw [my frame] (0,-1.5) -- (0,1.5);
\draw [my frame,dashed] (0,1.5) -- (0,2);
             
\filldraw [black] (0,-2) circle (1.5pt)
          (0,2) circle (1.5pt);
\end{scope}
\useasboundingbox (-2,-2) rectangle (9.5,5.5);
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The homomorphism $\pi_v$}
\label{fig:lcsproj}
\end{figure}
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[Composition law\!\!\!]]
The claim is 
\[
	d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_u,\pi_v)+
	d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_w)\geq
	d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_u,\pi_w),
\]
or by (\ref{d_dk}), equivalently,
\begin{align}\label{triangle}
\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(v)-p(u)\}+\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(v)\}
\geq\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(u)\}.
\end{align}
We first prove 
\begin{align}\label{triangle3}
\big(p(v)-p(u)\big) + \big(p(w)-p(v)\big)\geq p(w)-p(u)
\end{align}
for each $p\in \mathbf{D}_0$, as follows:
\begin{prooftree}
	\def\ \geq\ {\ \geq\ }
	\alwaysDoubleLine
	\Axiom$\big(p(v)-p(u)\big) + \big(p(w)-p(v)\big)\ \geq\  p(w)-p(u)$
	\UnaryInf$\big(p(w)-p(v)\big)+\big(p(v)-p(u)\big) \ \geq\  p(w)-p(u)$
	\UnaryInf$p(w)-p(v) \ \geq\  \big(p(w)-p(u)\big)-\big(p(v)-p(u)\big)$
\end{prooftree}
where the bottom expression is an instance of the
composition law for $-$. 
Thus, by taking $\sup$ in (\ref{triangle3}) 
with respect to $p$, we have
\begin{align}\label{triangle2}
	\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\big\{\big(p(v)-p(u)\big) +\big(p(w)-p(v)\big)\big\}
	\geq\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(u)\}.
\end{align}
On the other hand, we have 
\[
\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(v)-p(u)\} +\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(v)\}
\geq\big(p^\prime(v)-p^\prime(u)\big) +\big(p^\prime(w)-p^\prime(v)\big)
\]
for each $p^\prime\in \mathbf{D}_0$, and therefore
\[
\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(v)-p(u)\} +\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(v)\}\geq 
\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\big\{\big(p(v)-p(u)\big) +\big(p(w)-p(v)\big)\big\}.
\]
We combine this with (\ref{triangle2}) and obtain
(\ref{triangle}).
\item[[Identity law\!\!\!]]
The claim is 
\[
	0\geq
	d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_v),
\]
or by (\ref{d_dk}), equivalently,
\begin{align}\label{identity1}
0\geq\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(v)-p(v)\}.
\end{align}
First note that for each $p\in \mathbf{D}_0$
\begin{align}\label{identity2}
0\geq p(v)-p(v)
\end{align}
holds, as proved below:
\begin{prooftree}
	\def\ \geq\ {\ \geq\ }
	\alwaysDoubleLine
	\Axiom$0\ \geq\  p(v)-p(v)$
	\UnaryInf$p(v)\ \geq\  p(v)$
\end{prooftree}
Taking $\sup$ in (\ref{identity2}) with respect to $p$
yields (\ref{identity1}).
\end{description}
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[(i)\!\!\!]]
Since 
$[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0)$,
${\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0=\{\pi_a\}_{a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}$.
We claim that 
\[
{\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}\colon\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow{\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}
\]
given by
\[
\textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{A}}})\colon\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})\longrightarrow{\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0,\qquad \textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{A}}})(a)={\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}(a)=\pi_a
\quad (\forall a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}))
\]
as a map between the sets of points, is an isomorphism
(note that 
$\textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{A}}})=\pi_{[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]}$,
where $\pi_{[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]}$ is
the bijection defined in Lemma \ref{lcs2kcat_ob}).
By Proposition \ref{iso_ffb}, it suffices to show
that ${\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}$ is a fully faithful 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functor 
(because $\textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{A}}})$ is 
certainly bijective).
Therefore, we aim to show
\begin{align*}
d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
&=d_{{\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_a,\pi_b),
\end{align*}
or equivalently,
\begin{align*}
d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
&=\sup_{p\in [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0}\{p(b)-p(a)\}.
\end{align*}
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[\mbox{$d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
\leq \sup_{p\in [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0}\{p(b)-p(a)\}\!\!\!$}]]
Recall that $\overline{Y}(a)$ defined by
\[
\textup{Ob}(\overline{Y}(a))=\lambda b\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}).\,\textup{Hom}_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
\]
is an element of $[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$.
Therefore, 
\[
d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)-d_\mathcal{A}(a,a)=
\overline{Y}(a)(b)-\overline{Y}(a)(a)\leq 
\sup_{p\in [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0}\{p(b)-p(a)\}
\]
and it suffices to show 
\[
d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)\geq d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)-d_\mathcal{A}(a,a),
\]
but this follows from the identity law 
$0\geq d_\mathcal{A}(a,a)$ for $\mathcal{A}$ and 
the unit law and the monotonicity of $-$.
\item[[\mbox{$d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)
\geq \sup_{p\in [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0}\{p(b)-p(a)\}\!\!\!$}]]
It suffices to show 
\[
d_\mathcal{A}(a,b)\geq p(b)-p(a)
\]
for each $p\in [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$;
but this is nothing but the increasing condition for
$p$.
\end{description}
\item[[(ii)\!\!\!]]
Recall that 
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}=(\{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}},d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}})$,
where
\[
d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_w)
=\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(v)\}.
\]
Now, the $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex set
$[{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]=
(\{\pi_v\}_{v\in{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}},[{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0)$
is defined as follows: for all $q\in \overline{\mathbb{K}}^{\{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}}$,
\[
q\in [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0
\iff 
d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_w)\geq 
q(\pi_w)-q(\pi_v)\quad (\forall \pi_v,\pi_w\in \{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}).
\]
We prove that bijections
\begin{align*}
{\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})}\colon {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}\longrightarrow \{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}},\qquad &
{\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})}(v)=\pi_v \quad (\forall v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}),\\
{\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})}\colon \{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}\longrightarrow {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\qquad &
{\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})}(\pi_v)=v\quad (\forall \pi_v\in \{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}})
\end{align*}
define homomorphisms 
(note that ${\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})}=\pi_\mathbf{D}$,
where $\pi_\mathbf{D}$ is the bijection defined in the 
proof of Lemma \ref{lcs2kcat_ob}); 
in other words, there are maps
\begin{align*}
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\colon [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\longrightarrow \mathbf{D}_0,\quad&
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0(q)=\lambda v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}.\, q(\pi_v) \quad (\forall q\in [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0),\\
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})_0\colon\mathbf{D}_0\longrightarrow [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0,\quad  &
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})_0(p)=\lambda \pi_v\in \{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}.\,p(v)\quad (\forall p\in \mathbf{D}_0).
\end{align*}
To be more specific about the point, we have to verify
that the images of the above maps are 
contained in the codomains.
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[\mbox{$({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0([{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}, \overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0)\subseteq \mathbf{D}_0\!\!\!$}]]
First we prove that for all images 
$\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\in [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}, \overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$
of the co-Yoneda embedding, 
$({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\textup{Ob}(\overline{Y}(\pi_v))\big)$,
abbreviated as 
$({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)$, 
is an element of $\mathbf{D}_0$.
To see this, observe that
\begin{align*}
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)
&=\lambda w\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}.\,d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_w)\\
&=\lambda w\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}.\,\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(v)\},
\end{align*}
in other words, 
\begin{align*}
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)
&=\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p-p(v)\cdot\textbf{1}\}\\
&=\bigwedge_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p-p(v)\cdot\textbf{1}\}
\end{align*}
holds.
So the weight and order completeness conditions for
$\mathbf{D}$ assure 
$({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)\in\mathbf{D}_0$.

Now take arbitrary 
$q\in [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}, \overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$. 
We have
\[
d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_w)\geq 
q(\pi_w)-q(\pi_v)\quad (\forall \pi_v,\pi_w\in \{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}).
\]
The adjointness relation (for the SMC-CL 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$) yields
\[
d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_w)
+q(\pi_v)\geq 
q(\pi_w)\quad (\forall \pi_v,\pi_w\in \{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}),
\]
so, with respect to the coordinate-wise order on 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}^{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$
(also denoted by $\geq$), we obtain
\begin{align}\label{yoneda_q}
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)
+q(\pi_v)\cdot \textbf{1}\geq 
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0(q)\quad (\forall v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}).
\end{align}
Since by the weight completeness 
$({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)+q(\pi_v)\cdot \textbf{1}$
is an element of $\mathbf{D}_0$, so is 
\[
q^\prime=\inf_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}\left\{({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)+q(\pi_v)\cdot \textbf{1}\right\}=
\bigvee_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}\left\{({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)+q(\pi_v)\cdot \textbf{1}\right\}
\]
by the order completeness.
We claim that $q^\prime=({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0(q)$.
Because the definition of $q^\prime$ and (\ref{yoneda_q})
imply $q^\prime\geq ({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0(q)$,
it suffices to show that for each $v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$,
$q^\prime(v)\leq ({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0(q)(v)=q(\pi_v)$.
This is proved as follows:
\begin{align*}
q^\prime(v)
&=\left(\inf_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}\left\{({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)+q(\pi_v)\cdot \textbf{1}\right\}\right)(v)\\
&\leq \left(({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)+q(\pi_v)\cdot \textbf{1}\right)(v)\\
&=({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})_0\big(\overline{Y}(\pi_v)\big)(v)+(q(\pi_v)\cdot \textbf{1})(v)\\
&=d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_v)+q(\pi_v)\\
&\leq 0+q(\pi_v)\\
&=q(\pi_v).
\end{align*}
\item[[\mbox{$({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})_0(\mathbf{D}_0)\subseteq [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}, \overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\!\!\!$}]]
The condition for $({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})_0(p)$ 
to be an element of 
$[{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}, \overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$
is that 
\[
d_{{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}}(\pi_v,\pi_w)\geq 
({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})_0(p)(\pi_w)-({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})_0(p)(\pi_v)\quad (\forall \pi_v,\pi_w\in \{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}),
\]
which is equivalent to
\[
\sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0}\{p(w)-p(v)\}\geq 
p(w)-p(v)\quad (\forall v,w\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}),
\]
an obvious inequality.
\end{description}
Therefore, we have two homomorphisms
\begin{align*}
{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}&\colon [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\longrightarrow \mathbf{D},\\
{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime}&\colon \mathbf{D}\longrightarrow [{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}].
\end{align*}
Clearly
${\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})}\circ {\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})}=1_{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$ and
${\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})}\circ {\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}^\prime})}=1_{\{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}}$ hold, 
and they are isomorphisms.
\end{description}
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
\label{cor}
For any $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex set
$\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$, the pair
$\mathcal{D}=(\mathbf{D}_0,d_{\mathbf{D}_0})$
where $d_{\mathbf{D}_0}$ is a distance function on 
$\mathbf{D}_0$ given as the restriction of 
the sup-distance on 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}^{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$, is 
isomorphic to the presheaf category 
${\textup{Fun}({\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}})}$
of the $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-category
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}^\textup{op}$.
Conversely, the presheaf category
${\textup{Fun}(\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\overline{\mathbb{K}})}=([\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0,d_{[\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0})$
of any $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-category
$\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ 
gives rise to a 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex set
$[\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),[\mathcal{A}^\textup{op},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0)$.
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- thm-3: body_md -->
\label{mainthm_map}
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ 
and $\mathcal{B}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{B}),d_\mathcal{B})$ 
be $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-categories, and 
$\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$ 
and $\mathbf{E}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})},\mathbf{E}_0)$ 
be $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex sets.
Then the following hold:
\begin{enumerate}
\item
The map 
$[-,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\colon[\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}]_0\longrightarrow{\llbracket [\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}] \rrbracket}_0$
is bijective.
\item
The map 
${\llbracket -,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\colon{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\mathbf{E} \rrbracket}_0\longrightarrow[{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}]_0$
is bijective.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-3 -->
<!-- lem-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:cat2lcs_hom}
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ 
and $\mathcal{B}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{B}),d_\mathcal{B})$ 
be $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-categories and 
\[
F\colon \mathcal{A}\longrightarrow \mathcal{B}
\]
be a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functor.
Then there is a homomorphism of 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex sets 
\[
[F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\colon [\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\longrightarrow[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],
\]
defined as ${\textup{Ind}([F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}=\textup{Ob}(F)$.
<!-- end lem-3 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Since 
$[\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{B}),[\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0)$ and
$[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0)$,
a homomorphism from $[\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$
to $[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$ is given 
as a map from
${\textup{Ind}([\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}=\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})$ to 
${\textup{Ind}([\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}=\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{B})$,
such as $\textup{Ob}(F)$;
so at least the type matches.
What we have to check is that 
for all $p\in[\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$,
\begin{align}\label{nonexp_comp}
	p\circ \textup{Ob}(F)\colon \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})\overset{\textup{Ob}(F)}{\longrightarrow} \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{B})\overset{p}{\longrightarrow} \mathbb{K}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
\end{align}
is an element of 
$[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$, i.e., 
nonexpansive.
But (\ref{nonexp_comp}) is a composition of 
nonexpansive maps and so nonexpansive as well.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lem-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:lcs2cat_hom}
Let $\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$ 
and $\mathbf{E}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})},\mathbf{E}_0)$ 
be $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex sets and 
\[
\Phi\colon \mathbf{D}\longrightarrow \mathbf{E}
\]
be a homomorphism.
Then there is a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functor 
\[
{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\colon {\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\longrightarrow{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},
\]
defined as  
$\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})=\pi_\mathbf{D}\circ {\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}\circ \pi_\mathbf{E}^{-1}$, 
where $\pi_\mathbf{D}$ and $\pi_\mathbf{E}$ are the bijections 
defined in the proof of Lemma \ref{lcs2kcat_ob}.
<!-- end lem-4 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Since ${\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}=
\big({\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0,d_{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\big)$ 
and
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}=
\big({\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0,d_{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\big)$,
a $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functor from 
${\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$ to
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$ is given as a 
map from 
$\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})={\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0$ to
$\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})={\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0$, 
such as 
$\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})=\pi_\mathbf{D}\circ {\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}\circ \pi_\mathbf{E}^{-1}$:
\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}
\node (P1) at (3,2) {${\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0$} ;
\node (P2) at (3,0) {${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0$};
\node (P3) at (0,2) {${\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}$};
\node (P4) at (0,0) {${\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}$};
\draw
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[right] {$\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})$} (P2)
(P3) edge[my arrow] node[above] {$\pi_\mathbf{E}$} (P1)
(P4) edge[my arrow] node[below] {$\pi_\mathbf{D}$} (P2)
(P3) edge[my arrow] node[left] {${\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}$} (P4);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}
We adopt the notation
${\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0=\{\pi_w\}_{w\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}}$
and 
${\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0=\{\pi_v\}_{v\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}$;
then 
\[
\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})(\pi_w)=\pi_{{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}(w)}\quad (\forall \pi_w\in \{\pi_w\}_{w\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}}).
\]
We aim to show that 
\[
d_{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}(\pi_w,\pi_{w^\prime})
\geq d_{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}(\pi_{{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}(w)},\pi_{{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}(w^\prime)})\quad (\forall \pi_w,\pi_{w^\prime}\in \{\pi_w\}_{w\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}}),
\]
or equivalently 
(by (\ref{d_dk}) in the proof of Lemma 
\ref{lcs2kcat_ob}),
\[
\sup_{q\in \mathbf{E}_0}\{q(w^\prime)-q(w)\}
\geq \sup_{p\in \mathbf{D}_0} \left\{p\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}(w^\prime)-p\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}(w)\right\}\quad (\forall w,w^\prime\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}).
\]
Now recall that the condition for ${\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}$ to
define a homomorphism is that for all $p\in \mathbf{D}_0$,
$p\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}\in \mathbf{E}_0$; hence
\[
\{q(w^\prime)-q(w)\mid q\in \mathbf{E}_0\}\supseteq 
\left\{p\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}(w^\prime)-p\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}(w)\mid p\in \mathbf{D}_0\right\}\quad (\forall w,w^\prime\in {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})})
\]
and the proof is done.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[(i)\!\!\!]]
Since for each $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functor 
$F\colon\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}$,
$[F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$ is defined as
\[
{\textup{Ind}([F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}=\textup{Ob}(F),
\]
$[-,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$ is clearly injective.

To prove surjectivity of $[-,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$,
take an arbitrary homomorphism
\[
\Phi\colon[\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\longrightarrow[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}].
\]
Let 
$F_\Phi={\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}\circ{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\circ{\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}$,
where $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functors
${\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}$ and ${\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}$ are 
the isomorphisms defined in the proof of 
Theorem \ref{mainthm_ob} (i):
\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}
\node (P1) at (0,2) {$\mathcal{A}$} ;
\node (P2) at (3,2) {${\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$};
\node (P3) at (0,0) {$\mathcal{B}$};
\node (P4) at (3,0) {${\llbracket [\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$};
\draw
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[above] {${\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}$} (P2)
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[left] {$F_\Phi$} (P3)
(P2) edge[my arrow] node[right] {${\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$} (P4)
(P3) edge[my arrow] node[below] {${\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}$} (P4);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}
We claim that $[F_\Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]=\Phi$.
Recall
$\textup{Ob}({\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})={\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0=\{\pi_a\}_{a\in\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}$
and
\[
\textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{A}}})=\pi_{[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]}=\lambda a\in\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}).\,\pi_a \colon\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})\longrightarrow\textup{Ob}({\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}).
\]
Now we can conclude that
\begin{align*}
{\textup{Ind}([F_\Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}&=\textup{Ob}(F_\Phi)\\
&=\textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{B}}})^{-1}\circ\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})\circ\textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{A}}})\\
&=\textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{B}}})^{-1}\circ\pi_{[\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]}\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}\circ\pi_{[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]}^{-1}\circ\textup{Ob}({\eta_{\mathcal{A}}})\\
&=1_{\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{B})}\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}\circ1_{\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})}\\
&={\textup{Ind}(\Phi)},
\end{align*}
hence $[F_\Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]=\Phi$.
\item[[(ii)\!\!\!]]
Since for each homomorphism 
$\Phi\colon\mathbf{D}\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}$,
${\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$ is defined as
\[
\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})=\pi_\mathbf{D}\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}\circ\pi_\mathbf{E}^{-1},
\]
where $\pi_\mathbf{D}$ and $\pi_\mathbf{E}$ are fixed 
bijections, 
${\llbracket -,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$ is clearly injective.

To prove surjectivity of ${\llbracket -,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$,
take an arbitrary $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functor
\[
F\colon{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\longrightarrow{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}.
\]
Let 
$\Phi_F={\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}}\circ[F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\circ{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1}$,
or equivalently,
${\textup{Ind}(\Phi_F)}={\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})}^{-1}\circ{\textup{Ind}([F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}\circ{\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}})}$,
where homomorphisms
${\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}$ and ${\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}}$ are 
the isomorphisms defined in the proof of 
Theorem \ref{mainthm_ob} (ii):
\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}
\node (P1) at (0,2) {$[{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$} ;
\node (P2) at (3,2) {$\mathbf{D}$};
\node (P3) at (0,0) {$[{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$};
\node (P4) at (3,0) {$\mathbf{E}$};
\draw
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[above] {${\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}$} (P2)
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[left] {$[F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$} (P3)
(P2) edge[my arrow] node[right] {$\Phi_F$} (P4)
(P3) edge[my arrow] node[below] {${\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}}$} (P4);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}
We claim that ${\llbracket \Phi_F,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}=F$.
Recall 
${\textup{Ind}([{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}=\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})={\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}_0=\{\pi_v\}_{v\in{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}}$
and
\begin{align*}
{\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})}=\pi_\mathbf{D}=\lambda v\in{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}.\,\pi_v\colon {\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})}\longrightarrow {\textup{Ind}([{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}.
\end{align*}
Now we can conclude that
\begin{align*}
\textup{Ob}({\llbracket \Phi_F,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket})&=
\pi_\mathbf{D}\circ{\textup{Ind}(\Phi_F)}\circ\pi_\mathbf{E}^{-1}\\
&=\pi_\mathbf{D}\circ{\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}})}^{-1}\circ{\textup{Ind}([F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}\circ{\textup{Ind}({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}})}\circ\pi_\mathbf{E}^{-1}\\
&=1_{{\textup{Ind}([{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}}\circ{\textup{Ind}([F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}\circ 1_{{\textup{Ind}([{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}}\\
&={\textup{Ind}([F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}])}\\
&=\textup{Ob}(F),
\end{align*}
hence ${\llbracket \Phi_F,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}=F$.
\end{description}
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{mainthm_canord}
Let $\mathcal{A}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A}),d_\mathcal{A})$ and
$\mathcal{B}=(\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{B}),d_\mathcal{B})$ be 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-categories,
$\mathbf{D}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{D})},\mathbf{D}_0)$ and 
$\mathbf{E}=({\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})},\mathbf{E}_0)$ be 
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-extended L-convex sets,
$F,G\colon\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}$ be
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functors, and
$\Phi,\Psi\colon\mathbf{D}\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}$ be
homomorphisms.
Then the following hold:
\begin{enumerate}
\item
$F\Rightarrow G$ if and only if
$[F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\Rightarrow[G,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$.
\item
$\Phi\Rightarrow\Psi$ if and only if 
${\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket \Psi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
\begin{description}[font=\normalfont]
\item[[(i), the ``only if'' part\!\!\!]]
Since 
$[F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],[G,\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\colon[\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\longrightarrow[\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$,
we aim to prove that for all 
$q\in[\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$,
\[
q\circ \textup{Ob}(F)(a)\geq q\circ \textup{Ob}(G)(a) \quad(\forall a\in\textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})).
\]
In fact, when we view $q$ as a
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functor, it is equivalent to
$q\circ F\Rightarrow q\circ G$,
which follows from the fact that $\Rightarrow$ on
$\overline{\mathbb{K}}$-functors is reflexive and
preserved under composition.
\item[[(ii), the ``only if'' part\!\!\!]]
Since 
${\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},{\llbracket \Psi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\colon{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\longrightarrow{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$,
we aim to prove that for all
$\pi_w\in{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$,
\[
d_{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}(\pi_{{\textup{Ind}(\Phi)}(w)},\pi_{{\textup{Ind}(\Psi)}(w)})=\sup_{p\in\mathbf{D}_0}\{\Psi_0(p)(w)-\Phi_0(p)(w)\}\leq0.
\]
Because 
$0\geq\Psi_0(p)(w)-\Phi_0(p)(w)\iff\Phi_0(p)(w)\geq\Psi_0(p)(w)$ 
by
\begin{prooftree}
	\def\ \geq\ {\ \geq\ }
	\alwaysDoubleLine
	\Axiom$0\ \geq\ \Psi_0(p)(w)-\Phi_0(p)(w)$
	\UnaryInf$\Phi_0(p)(w)\ \geq\  \Psi_0(p)(w)$
\end{prooftree}
it suffices to show that
\[
\Phi_0(p)\geq\Psi_0(p)
\]
holds in $\mathbf{E}_0$ for all $p\in\mathbf{D}_0$, but this is 
nothing but the condition for $\Phi\Rightarrow\Psi$.
\item[[(i), the ``if'' part\!\!\!]]
The ``only if'' part of (ii) implies that 
${\llbracket [F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket [G,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$
holds.
By the proof of Theorem \ref{mainthm_map} (i),
we have the following commutative diagram:
\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}
\node (P1) at (0,2) {$\mathcal{A}$} ;
\node (P2) at (3,2) {${\llbracket [\mathcal{A},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$};
\node (P3) at (0,0) {$\mathcal{B}$};
\node (P4) at (3,0) {${\llbracket [\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$};
\draw
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[above] {${\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}$} (P2)
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[left] {$F$} (P3)
(P2) edge[my arrow] node[right] {${\llbracket [F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}$} (P4)
(P3) edge[my arrow] node[below] {${\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}$} (P4);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}
and similarly for $G$. 
In equations, 
\begin{align*}
F&={\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}\circ{\llbracket [F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\circ{\eta_{\mathcal{A}}},\\
G&={\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}\circ{\llbracket [G,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\circ{\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}.
\end{align*}
Since ${\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}$, ${\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}$ are
isomorphisms, we have
\begin{align*}
d_\mathcal{B}(F(a),G(a))&=
d_\mathcal{B}({\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}\circ{\llbracket [F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\circ{\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}(a),{\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}\circ{\llbracket [G,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}\circ{\eta_{\mathcal{A}}}(a))\\
&=d_\mathcal{B}({\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}\circ{\llbracket [F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}(\pi_a),{\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}\circ{\llbracket [G,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}(\pi_a))\\
&=d_{\llbracket [\mathcal{B},\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}({\llbracket [F,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}(\pi_a),{\llbracket [G,\overline{\mathbb{K}}],\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket}(\pi_a))\\
&\leq 0
\end{align*}
for all $a\in \textup{Ob}(\mathcal{A})$. 
\item[[(ii), the ``if'' part\!\!\!]]
The ``only if'' part of (i) implies that 
$[{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\Rightarrow[{\llbracket \Psi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$
holds.
By the proof of Theorem \ref{mainthm_map} (ii),
we have the following commutative diagram:
\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}
\node (P1) at (0,2) {$[{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$} ;
\node (P2) at (3,2) {$\mathbf{D}$};
\node (P3) at (0,0) {$[{\llbracket \mathbf{E},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$};
\node (P4) at (3,0) {$\mathbf{E}$};
\draw
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[above] {${\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}$} (P2)
(P1) edge[my arrow] node[left] {$[{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]$} (P3)
(P2) edge[my arrow] node[right] {$\Phi$} (P4)
(P3) edge[my arrow] node[below] {${\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}}$} (P4);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}
and similarly for $\Psi$. 
In equations,
\begin{align*}
\Phi&={\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}}\circ[{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\circ{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1},\\
\Psi&={\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}}\circ[{\llbracket \Psi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]\circ{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1}.
\end{align*}
We have
\begin{align*}
\Phi_0(p)(w)&=({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}})_0\circ[{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\circ({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)(w)\\
&=({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{E}}})_0\big([{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\circ({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)\big)(w)\\
&=\big(\lambda w^\prime\in{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}.\,[{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\circ({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)(\pi_{w^\prime})\big)(w)\\
&=[{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\circ({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)(\pi_w)\\
&=[{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\big(({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)\big)(\pi_w)
\end{align*}
and 
\[
\Psi_0(p)(w)=[{\llbracket \Psi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\big(({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)\big)(\pi_w)
\]
for all $p\in\mathbf{D}_0$ and $w\in{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}$.
Since 
$({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)\in[{\llbracket \mathbf{D},\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0$,
we have 
\[
[{\llbracket \Phi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\big(({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)\big)(\pi_w)\geq
[{\llbracket \Psi,\overline{\mathbb{K}} \rrbracket},\overline{\mathbb{K}}]_0\big(({\varepsilon_{\mathbf{D}}}^{-1})_0(p)\big)(\pi_w).
\]
Therefore we obtain
$\Phi(p)(w)\geq \Psi(p)(w)$
for all $p\in\mathbf{D}_0$ and $w\in{\textup{Ind}(\mathbf{E})}$;
so $\Phi(p)\geq\Psi(p)$ for all $p\in\mathbf{D}_0$;
so $\Phi\Rightarrow\Psi$.
\end{description}
